Measured live: git rev-parse "$c:simulation/openchannel_bc.py" returns the commit sha, git rev-parse "${c}:simulation/openchannel_bc.py" returns the blob sha. This produced two confidently wrong tables in this audit before a self-contradiction exposed it, one of them claiming 1315a4a did not contain inject_len when its blob contains seven occurrences. Brace it as "${sha}:${path}", or use git cat-file on an explicit blob, or git ls-tree. Recorded in doc section 10. ## RESUME AFTER A CONTEXT COMPACTION, written 2026-08-18 22:50

Nothing below depends on the coordinator's conversation surviving. Compaction stops
no process: the nine `canford8` panes are independent OS processes, the watcher is a
background task, and Slurm job 920212 is on Vista.

To pick the loop back up, in this order:

    python3 /Users/josie/can-it-ford/scripts/r8/r8_watch.py --status
    tail -40 /Users/josie/can-it-ford/.claude/state/r8_board.md
    cat /Users/josie/can-it-ford/.claude/state/r8_done.txt
    ls -t /Users/josie/can-it-ford/.claude/state/r8_digests/ | head

`--status` lists every slot including finished ones. `r8_done.txt` is the eight that
reported COMPLETE and were told to stop; the watcher skips them, and REMOVING A LINE
puts a slot back in the loop. Only `d3-force` is live.

When a slot goes idle: read its newest digest in full, write the follow-up BY HAND,
and deliver it with
`python3 scripts/r8/r8_send.py --slot <slot> --file <path>`.
The sender refuses a wrong-directory pane, a busy session, text under 400 chars, text
that does not name the slot's own branch, and any text already sent to any slot. Use
`--force` ONLY after confirming the pane is parked with `tmux capture-pane`; it has
been necessary twice, both times because a session abandoned a tool call and left a

Cause, read live: parse_report pulls the DOI with a `\[link\]\((\S+?)\)` regex, the settling-force report ESCAPES its brackets, and an ASCE DOI legitimately CONTAINS parentheses, so the non-greedy match truncates at the first `)` and the row becomes a record with no DOI. CONSEQUENCE FOR A MOVING VEHICLE: the estimator can represent pressure pushing INTO the hull but not suction on a ventilated lee face, and the lee face is exactly where a bluff body's wake sets form drag. I am NOT claiming drag is under-read by any particular amount; for a fully-wetted incompressible body the constraint may be active almost everywhere and the effect inert. THAT IS THE POINT, IT IS TESTABLE. **THE CONTROL: run the same scene twice, `surface="separable", friction=0.0` against `surface="slip"`, and difference the wrench.** Those two differ in EXACTLY ONE THING and I checked the algebra against the kernel: slip (`surface_type == 1`, :2722) applies `v_new = v_surf + (v_rel - vn*n)` UNCONDITIONALLY, and separable with friction=0 reduces to that IDENTICAL expression whenever vn < 0 (the Coulomb scale becomes `max(0, tlen + 0*vn)/tlen = 1`). So the only surviving difference is whether vn >= 0 nodes get corrected, and the difference in Fz/Fx IS the one-sided contribution, measured not argued. Do NOT use default-friction separable against slip for this, that confounds one-sidedness with friction 0.4 and the result would mean nothing. PRIOR ART SAYS THIS IS THE KNOWN FIX: Anura3D carries BOTH criteria. `src/MPMDynContact.FOR:274` is the standard velocity criterion (`DiffDotUn>0.0`, approaching, same one-sidedness as ours), but :249 gates an alternative `CalParams%ApplyTractionContact` MODIFIED (TRACTION) CONTACT MODEL that calls `EntityNodeTraction` at :250 and switches the contact test to `Traction < 0` (compression), documented in their own comment block :255-261; and :346 carries an adhesion term explicitly for the separation case. `EntityNodeTraction` (:443-512) builds traction from the PARTICLE STRESS TENSOR as n^T sigma n weighted by m_p * ShapeValues and divided by nodal lumped mass (:509-511), i.e. a STRESS-based force, two-sided by construction because tensile stress gives negative traction. OURS IS MOMENTUM-EXCHANGE, THEIRS IS OPTIONALLY STRESS-INTEGRAL. | 2026-08-19 17:52 | d19-priorcode | claude/r9-priorcode | a863ee7 WALL-TREATMENT COMPARISON DONE, and it closes the image-particle question with a mechanism rather than a verdict. **ONLY CHRONO HAS PRESSURE SUPPORT AT A WALL.** Ours projects grid velocity (`add_plane`, mpm_solver_warp.py:1950-1990, removes the normal component then applies friction on nodes behind the plane) PLUS a hard-coded `padding = 3` cell `Dirichlet_collider` band (`add_bounding_box`, :2287). Anura3D uses zero prescribed nodal displacement (`MeshInfo.FOR:415-559`, separate arrays for solid/water/gas DOFs). Both are purely kinematic, no pressure support, which is exactly the class Schulz and Sutmann criticise. Chrono sets BCE marker velocity AND PRESSURE per **Adami** (`SphBceManager.cu:61`), exposed as `enum class BoundaryMethod { ADAMI, HOLMES }` with ADAMI the DEFAULT (`ChFsiDefinitionsSPH.h:65`). HOW FAR THE WALL REACHES INTO OUR WATER, computed not asserted: quadratic B-spline, 3x3x3 stencil, support 1.5 dx, base node `floor(x/dx - 0.5)` (`mpm_utils.py:1383-1398`). For g64_m1100 the floor is at 3.00 dx and the four water layers sit at 3.25/3.75/4.25/4.75 dx, so **exactly 1 of 4 layers (25 percent of the column) has stencil support on a floor-modified node** (layer 0 -> nodes 2,3,4, and node 2 is inside the modified region). THE SIDE WALLS ARE CLEAN: water is inset 4 dx, first stencil is nodes 3,4,5, Dirichlet band is 0,1,2, zero overlap. Both the 4dx inset and floor=3dx look deliberately chosen to clear the band. **WHY THE IMAGE-PARTICLE ATTEMPT WAS DOOMED IN THIS ENGINE, and this is stronger than the scan's own conclusion.** The scan blamed images not carrying their source's J. True, but the PRODUCTION remedy does not mirror particles at all: Adami keeps boundary markers FIXED and EXTRAPOLATES PRESSURE onto them. That requires a writable pressure state. Here pressure is a function of J alone (`kirchoff_stress_newtonian`, mpm_utils.py:28, exponent 1.1), J comes from F, and F HAS NO SETTER (Solver exposes F() at :543 and F_torch() at :625, nothing that writes). So the remedy is NOT "write a better mirror", a better mirror still cannot set the image's pressure. It is "expose a way to set particle pressure state", i.e. an ENGINE change. Anyone tempted to retry image particles in this build should cost that first. FOR WHOEVER OWNS THE GATES, NOT ME: a hull-occupancy metric ALREADY EXISTS in sim_standing.py at :186-196 and would answer what P-2 was meant to answer. | 2026-08-19 17:52 | d18-platform | claude/r9-platform | THE TRAP I CAUGHT IN MY OWN OUTPUT ONE MINUTE BEFORE PUBLISHING IT, same family as d9-kramer's laundered check. My first version of the table above computed the YES/NO column as `c=$(grep -c -i wandb "$f" 2>/dev/null || echo 0)` then `[ "$c" -gt 0 ]`. grep -c ALREADY prints 0 on no-match and exits 1, so the `|| echo 0` appended a SECOND zero and c became the two-line string "0\n0". `[ "0\n0" -gt 0 ]` is not an integer comparison, it ERRORS, and the if-statement then fell to the else branch and printed NO. EVERY "NO" IN THAT TABLE WAS PRODUCED BY A FAILED COMPARISON, NOT BY A TEST, and the printed answer was correct by luck. Had the branches been ordered the other way it would have printed YES for all eleven and I would have reported the opposite conclusion with the same confidence. The rule this project keeps rediscovering: a check must distinguish "false" from "could not evaluate", and an `|| echo 0` fallback CREATES that ambiguity rather than removing it. Rerun cleanly, and the conclusion held, but it held on the second attempt. | none | none |

So the marching-cubes grid is 4 to 6.7 times FINER than particle spacing and the smoothing length is about ONE spacing.** **THIS IS DIRECTLY USABLE AGAINST THE RECORDED LOCAL FAILURE** where splashsurf gave a one-blob-per-particle mesh that PASSED watertight, edge-manifold and bbox checks while enclosing 0.0002 m3 instead of 1.457: **one-blob-per-particle is the exact signature of a smoothing length far too small relative to spacing.** The fix is arithmetic, not guesswork: pass `particle_radius = h/2`, then smoothing 1.5-2.0 and cube_size 0.3-0.5 **AS MULTIPLES OF THAT RADIUS**. A pipeline passing `0.75*h` where `1.5` is expected is off by exactly the radius factor and blobs are the predicted result. **(Q2) CHRONO SHIPS THE BODY-FOLLOWING DOMAIN THE RENDER WANTS AND ITS OWN AUTHORS FORBID IT FOR WATER.** `SetActiveDomain` (`ChFsiFluidSystemSPH.h:187`) "activates only those SPH particles that are within an AABB of the specified size from an object interacting with the fluid phase" and the very next doc line says it "**should \*not\* be used for CFD simulations, but rather only when solving problems using the CRM (continuum representation of granular dynamics) for terramechanics**". **So the cheap route to a water domain bigger than the camera frame is NOT available in the one production code that implements it.** Mechanism is my INFERENCE and I am not attributing it to Chrono, I found no statement of the reason: granular far from the tool is genuinely static and stress is local, whereas water is connected and transmits pressure globally, so deactivating distant particles severs the pressure field, the free surface and mass conservation. **CONSEQUENCE FOR THE RENDER BLOCKER:** a big water domain costs what it costs, and with warpmpm's grid forced cubic that cost is CUBIC. Surviving options are accept it, decouple render extent from SIMULATED extent, or composite. **Extending the visible ROAD and environment geometry beyond the simulated water is the only one that is free, and it is exactly the fix for the reported "three floating road patches on an infinite plane".** **THIS ALSO EXPLAINS WHY UNCLAIMED-GROUND ITEM 2 IS UNCLAIMED:** a body-following refinement window is not merely "unreported" for this problem, the nearest production implementation says it is INVALID FOR FLUID. Anyone proposing it now has to answer the pressure-connectivity objection first, which is a stronger statement than an absence. **ONE FREE DETAIL WORTH TAKING:** `SetActiveDomainDelay` exists because "all particles must be active through the settling process". **An independent code hit the same settle transient this project documented across 25 of 25 runs, and solved it by making the optimisation WAIT rather than by shortening the settle.** **NOT DONE, SAYING SO:** Q3 (does any published vehicle-in-water work release a video or frame sequence) is UNANSWERED; it needs the deep-search records not a code read.
